 condition: it neither replaces the nonlinear tail energy by a moment nor
 assumes regular variation.
 \end{remark}
+
+The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
+potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
+difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
+difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
+nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
+\label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
+Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
+\(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
+ m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
+ \qquad
+ \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
+\end{equation}
+For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
+\[
+ \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
+ V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
+ F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
+\]
+and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
+\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
+write
+\[
+ C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
+             =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
+\]
+with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
+ A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
+integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
+densities
+\[
+ k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
+                    A_s^\lambda(x/s).
+\]
+The nonnegative raw-tail defect energy
+\begin{align}
+ \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
+ &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
+ \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
+\end{align}
+is finite, and, with
+\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+  +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
+\end{equation}
+In particular, law by law,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
+ s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
+ \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
+ s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
+\end{equation}
+No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
+order \(r\), is required.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
+\(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
+\(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
+Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
+translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
+\begin{align}
+ &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
+ +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
+  \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,                                    
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
+ &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
+ +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le C_r|z|^j,qquad 1\le j\le r,                              
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
+\end{align}
+where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
+zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
+derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
+\(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
+to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
+\eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
+
+Set
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
+\]
+Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
+and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
+ q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
+minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
+\(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
+\[
+ s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
+ \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
+\]
+The same last estimate gives
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
+ \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+
+Translation preserves mass, so
+\[
+ V_s^\lambda\ge0,qquad
+ \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
+\]
+Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
+derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Since
+\(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=o(1)\), these facts and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} prove the asserted positivity and
+normalization of \(A_s^\lambda\).  Finiteness of the entropy terms follows
+as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}: the
+stable translation entropy is bounded by
+\(C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\), which is integrable under the finite first
+moment implied by \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}.
+
+It remains to separate the leading modes from two different tail
+backgrounds.  We record the needed estimate directly.  Put
+\(\epsilon=s^{-r}\), \(U_\lambda=\epsilon B_r^\lambda\), and abbreviate
+\(A_\nu=A_s^\nu\), \(A_\eta=A_s^\eta\).  All these backgrounds, as well as
+\(q_s^\lambda\), are bounded below by a positive constant for large \(s\).
+For the last assertion, the bounded log-gradient of \(p_1\) and Jensen's
+inequality give
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)\ge
+ \exp\{-\|\nabla\log p_1\|_\infty\EE_\lambda|X|/s\}\longrightarrow1
+\]
+uniformly in \(y\).  Since
+\(\int(V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon)\), the mean-value
+theorem for
+\(f(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\), together with
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}, yields
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders}
+ \int f(q_s^\nu,q_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int f(A_\nu+U_\nu,A_\eta+U_\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+  +o(\epsilon^2).
+\end{equation}
+Explicitly, along the joining segments both first derivatives of \(f\) are
+bounded by
+\(C\{\epsilon+V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta\}\); integration bounds the error by
+\[
+ C\epsilon(\|R_s^\nu\|_1+\|R_s^\eta\|_1)
+ +C(\|R_s^\nu\|_\infty+\|R_s^\eta\|_\infty)
+   (\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

+++ b/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex
@@ -506,3 +506,218 @@
 condition: it neither replaces the nonlinear tail energy by a moment nor
 assumes regular variation.
 \end{remark}
+
+The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
+potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
+difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
+difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
+nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
+\label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
+Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
+\(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
+ m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
+ \qquad
+ \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
+\end{equation}
+For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
+\[
+ \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
+ V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
+ F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
+\]
+and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
+\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
+write
+\[
+ C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
+             =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
+\]
+with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
+ A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
+integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
+densities
+\[
+ k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
+                    A_s^\lambda(x/s).
+\]
+The nonnegative raw-tail defect energy
+\begin{align}
+ \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
+ &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
+ \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
+\end{align}
+is finite, and, with
+\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+  +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
+\end{equation}
+In particular, law by law,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
+ s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
+ \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
+ s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
+\end{equation}
+No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
+order \(r\), is required.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
+\(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
+\(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
+Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
+translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
+\begin{align}
+ &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
+ +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
+  \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,                                    
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
+ &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
+ +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le C_r|z|^j,qquad 1\le j\le r,                              
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
+\end{align}
+where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
+zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
+derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
+\(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
+to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
+\eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
+
+Set
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
+\]
+Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
+and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
+ q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
+minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
+\(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
+\[
+ s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
+ \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
+\]
+The same last estimate gives
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
+ \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+
+Translation preserves mass, so
+\[
+ V_s^\lambda\ge0,qquad
+ \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
+\]
+Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
+derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Since
+\(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=o(1)\), these facts and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} prove the asserted positivity and
+normalization of \(A_s^\lambda\).  Finiteness of the entropy terms follows
+as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}: the
+stable translation entropy is bounded by
+\(C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\), which is integrable under the finite first
+moment implied by \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}.
+
+It remains to separate the leading modes from two different tail
+backgrounds.  We record the needed estimate directly.  Put
+\(\epsilon=s^{-r}\), \(U_\lambda=\epsilon B_r^\lambda\), and abbreviate
+\(A_\nu=A_s^\nu\), \(A_\eta=A_s^\eta\).  All these backgrounds, as well as
+\(q_s^\lambda\), are bounded below by a positive constant for large \(s\).
+For the last assertion, the bounded log-gradient of \(p_1\) and Jensen's
+inequality give
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)\ge
+ \exp\{-\|\nabla\log p_1\|_\infty\EE_\lambda|X|/s\}\longrightarrow1
+\]
+uniformly in \(y\).  Since
+\(\int(V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon)\), the mean-value
+theorem for
+\(f(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\), together with
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}, yields
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders}
+ \int f(q_s^\nu,q_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int f(A_\nu+U_\nu,A_\eta+U_\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+  +o(\epsilon^2).
+\end{equation}
+Explicitly, along the joining segments both first derivatives of \(f\) are
+bounded by
+\(C\{\epsilon+V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta\}\); integration bounds the error by
+\[
+ C\epsilon(\|R_s^\nu\|_1+\|R_s^\eta\|_1)
+ +C(\|R_s^\nu\|_\infty+\|R_s^\eta\|_\infty)
+   (\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

 assumes regular variation.
 \end{remark}
+
+The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
+potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
+difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
+difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
+nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
+\label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
+Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
+\(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
+ m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
+ \qquad
+ \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
+\end{equation}
+For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
+\[
+ \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
+ V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
+ F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
+\]
+and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
+\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
+write
+\[
+ C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
+             =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
+\]
+with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
+ A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
+integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
+densities
+\[
+ k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
+                    A_s^\lambda(x/s).
+\]
+The nonnegative raw-tail defect energy
+\begin{align}
+ \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
+ &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
+ \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
+\end{align}
+is finite, and, with
+\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+  +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
+\end{equation}
+In particular, law by law,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
+ s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
+ \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
+ s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
+\end{equation}
+No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
+order \(r\), is required.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
+\(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
+\(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
+Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
+translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
+\begin{align}
+ &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
+ +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
+  \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,                                    
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
+ &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
+ +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le C_r|z|^j,qquad 1\le j\le r,                              
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
+\end{align}
+where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
+zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
+derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
+\(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
+to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
+\eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
+
+Set
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
+\]
+Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
+and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
+ q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
+minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
+\(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
+\[
+ s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
+ \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
+\]
+The same last estimate gives
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
+ \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+
+Translation preserves mass, so
+\[
+ V_s^\lambda\ge0,qquad
+ \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
+\]
+Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
+derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Since
+\(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=o(1)\), these facts and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} prove the asserted positivity and
+normalization of \(A_s^\lambda\).  Finiteness of the entropy terms follows
+as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}: the
+stable translation entropy is bounded by
+\(C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\), which is integrable under the finite first
+moment implied by \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}.
+
+It remains to separate the leading modes from two different tail
+backgrounds.  We record the needed estimate directly.  Put
+\(\epsilon=s^{-r}\), \(U_\lambda=\epsilon B_r^\lambda\), and abbreviate
+\(A_\nu=A_s^\nu\), \(A_\eta=A_s^\eta\).  All these backgrounds, as well as
+\(q_s^\lambda\), are bounded below by a positive constant for large \(s\).
+For the last assertion, the bounded log-gradient of \(p_1\) and Jensen's
+inequality give
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)\ge
+ \exp\{-\|\nabla\log p_1\|_\infty\EE_\lambda|X|/s\}\longrightarrow1
+\]
+uniformly in \(y\).  Since
+\(\int(V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon)\), the mean-value
+theorem for
+\(f(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\), together with
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}, yields
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders}
+ \int f(q_s^\nu,q_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int f(A_\nu+U_\nu,A_\eta+U_\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+  +o(\epsilon^2).
+\end{equation}
+Explicitly, along the joining segments both first derivatives of \(f\) are
+bounded by
+\(C\{\epsilon+V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta\}\); integration bounds the error by
+\[
+ C\epsilon(\|R_s^\nu\|_1+\|R_s^\eta\|_1)
+ +C(\|R_s^\nu\|_\infty+\|R_s^\eta\|_\infty)
+   (\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

Our increment is the heavy-tailed extension.  For \(0<\alpha<2\), the
Gaussian/Hermite and exponential-tail argument is replaced by a critical
stable translate-quotient estimate.  This determines the weakest uniform
complete-moment hypothesis, proves its endpoint with a little-\(o\)
remainder, and gives analytic counterexamples below it in the non-Hilbert
regime.  The mechanism holds at every first unmatched tensor order
\(r\ge1\).  With
\[
 q_{r,\alpha,d}=\min\left\{2,1+\frac{2r}{d+\alpha}\right\},
 \qquad
 p_{r,\alpha,d}=\frac{2r}{q_{r,\alpha,d}},
\]
Theorem~\ref{thm:all-order-stable-first-unmatched-moment} gives the
\(s^{-2r}\) quadratic tensor coefficient for two arbitrary inputs matching
all lower moments, under the endpoint \(p_{r,\alpha,d}\)-moment, and proves
that no smaller absolute-moment exponent works uniformly over the complete
moment class: for \(d+\alpha>2r\) this is analytic uniform sharpness on
pairs with a finite nonzero order-\(r\) tensor, whereas for
\(d+\alpha\le2r\) the value \(p_{r,\alpha,d}=r\) is only coefficient-domain
minimality below order \(r\).  The exact finite-covariance interface is instead supplied
by Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}.  For a centred law put
\[
 V_s^\nu(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
 \frac{p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y-x/s)}{p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y)}\,\nu(\dd x).
\]
Then, without regular variation, absolute continuity, or a moment above
order two,
\[
 H_{\alpha,d}(s)=\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d}(\Sigma)s^{-4}
 +\int\Phi(V_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-4}).
\]
Thus the covariance coefficient is attained exactly when the nonlinear raw
tail energy is \(o(s^{-4})\).  When \(d+\alpha\le4\), the uniform exponent
is two and this tail condition follows from finite covariance; when
\(d+\alpha>4\), it is the additional law-by-law condition not expressible by
the uniform moment exponent alone.  The infinite
critical-moment boundary is genuinely different: Theorem
\ref{thm:critical-vague-tail-nonidentifiability} constructs two laws with the
same smooth multivariate regularly varying tail data and the same covariance,
one attaining the covariance coefficient and the other having unbounded
scaled entropy along a subsequence.
Theorem~\ref{thm:covariance-proxy-defect-decomposition} gives an
asymptotically exact
finite-covariance characterization of this distinction.  If
\(k_{\Sigma,t}\) denotes the positive Poisson density obtained by adjoining
the normalized quadratic mode \(t^{-2}b_\Sigma\) to the matched reference,
then
\[
 t^4H_d(t)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
 +t^4D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|k_{\Sigma,t})+o(1).
\]
Thus attainment of the covariance coefficient is equivalent to the

where \(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}\) is the strictly positive quadratic

For an individual centred law with finite covariance \(\Sigma\), define
\[
 V_s^\nu(y):=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
 \frac{p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y-x/s)}
      {p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y)}\,\nu(\dd x).
\]
Then the exact law-by-law decomposition is
\[
 H_{\alpha,d}(s)=\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d}(\Sigma)s^{-4}
 +\int_{\RR^d}\Phi(V_s^\nu(y))\,\Omega_{\alpha,d}(\dd y)
 +o(s^{-4}),
\]
and hence
\[
 s^4H_{\alpha,d}(s)\longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d}(\Sigma)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 s^4\int_{\RR^d}\Phi(V_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\longrightarrow0.
\]
This clause assumes no regular variation, absolute continuity, or moment
above order two.

\begin{maintheorem}{Domain and endpoint closure for Cauchy relative entropy}
\label{thm:main-C}
Let \(\nu\) be a probability measure on \(\RR\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and
finite centred second moment.  Put
\[
\begin{aligned}
h_t&=P_t*\nu,\\
g_t&=P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),\\

 succeeded in 227ms:
Chen and Niles-Weed proved exact first unmatched moment relative-entropy
asymptotics under Gaussian smoothing, in every dimension and at every order,
assuming sub-Gaussian tails
\cite{ChenNilesWeed2022SmoothedWasserstein}.  We extend this mechanism to
the heavy-tailed isotropic strictly \(\alpha\)-stable kernels,
\(0<\alpha<2\), which exclude the Gaussian endpoint, and replace their
strong tail hypothesis by a far weaker, optimal complete-moment condition.
If two
laws on \(\RR^d\) match all tensor moments below the integer order \(r\ge1\),
have a nonzero order-\(r\) difference \(\Delta_r\), and have finite
\(p_{r,\alpha,d}\)th absolute moments, where
\[
 p_{r,\alpha,d}=\max\left\{r,
 \frac{2r(d+\alpha)}{d+\alpha+2r}\right\},
\]
then
\[
 D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}+o(s^{-2r}).
\]
The coefficient is an orthogonally invariant positive-definite quadratic
form on symmetric order-\(r\) tensors.  The endpoint moment is sufficient.
When \(d+\alpha>2r\), every exponent
\(\rho\in[r,p_{r,\alpha,d})\) fails uniformly even for pairs with finite
nonzero \(\Delta_r\); when \(d+\alpha\le2r\), the threshold equals \(r\), and
smaller exponents fail only to define \(\Delta_r\) throughout the moment
class.  Thus the contribution is the heavy-tailed critical exponent,
endpoint sufficiency, and uniform sharpness, not the Gaussian quadratic
mechanism itself.

At covariance order we also give a law-by-law decomposition under finite
covariance alone: the universal quadratic term plus a nonnegative nonlinear
energy of the unexpanded tail beyond the smoothing scale.  For the Cauchy
kernel, the Cayley transform additionally yields the exact equivalence between
finite variance and bounded \(t^4\) relative entropy, an all-order finite
Laurent rule for the subsequent coefficients, and a fully justified
nonlocal Bregman tail identity for the moving matched quotient.
\end{abstract}

 \end{remark}
+
+The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
+potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
+difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
+difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
+nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
+\label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
+Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
+\(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
+ m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
+ \qquad
+ \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
+\end{equation}
+For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
+\[
+ \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
+ V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
+ F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
+\]
+and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
+\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
+write
+\[
+ C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
+             =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
+\]
+with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
+ A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
+integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
+densities
+\[
+ k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
+                    A_s^\lambda(x/s).
+\]
+The nonnegative raw-tail defect energy
+\begin{align}
+ \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
+ &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
+ \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
+\end{align}
+is finite, and, with
+\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+  +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
+\end{equation}
+In particular, law by law,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
+ s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
+ \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
+ s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
+\end{equation}
+No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
+order \(r\), is required.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
+\(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
+\(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
+Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
+translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
+\begin{align}
+ &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
+ +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
+  \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,                                    
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
+ &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
+ +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le C_r|z|^j,qquad 1\le j\le r,                              
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
+\end{align}
+where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
+zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
+derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
+\(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
+to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
+\eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
+
+Set
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
+\]
+Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
+and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
+ q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
+minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
+\(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
+\[
+ s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
+ \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
+\]
+The same last estimate gives
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
+ \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+
+Translation preserves mass, so
+\[
+ V_s^\lambda\ge0,qquad
+ \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
+\]
+Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
+derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Since
+\(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=o(1)\), these facts and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} prove the asserted positivity and
+normalization of \(A_s^\lambda\).  Finiteness of the entropy terms follows
+as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}: the
+stable translation entropy is bounded by
+\(C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\), which is integrable under the finite first
+moment implied by \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}.
+
+It remains to separate the leading modes from two different tail
+backgrounds.  We record the needed estimate directly.  Put
+\(\epsilon=s^{-r}\), \(U_\lambda=\epsilon B_r^\lambda\), and abbreviate
+\(A_\nu=A_s^\nu\), \(A_\eta=A_s^\eta\).  All these backgrounds, as well as
+\(q_s^\lambda\), are bounded below by a positive constant for large \(s\).
+For the last assertion, the bounded log-gradient of \(p_1\) and Jensen's
+inequality give
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)\ge
+ \exp\{-\|\nabla\log p_1\|_\infty\EE_\lambda|X|/s\}\longrightarrow1
+\]
+uniformly in \(y\).  Since
+\(\int(V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon)\), the mean-value
+theorem for
+\(f(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\), together with
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}, yields
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders}
+ \int f(q_s^\nu,q_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int f(A_\nu+U_\nu,A_\eta+U_\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+  +o(\epsilon^2).
+\end{equation}
+Explicitly, along the joining segments both first derivatives of \(f\) are
+bounded by
+\(C\{\epsilon+V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta\}\); integration bounds the error by
+\[
+ C\epsilon(\|R_s^\nu\|_1+\|R_s^\eta\|_1)
+ +C(\|R_s^\nu\|_\infty+\|R_s^\eta\|_\infty)
+   (\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

+\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
+\label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
+Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
+\(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
+ m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
+ \qquad
+ \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
+\end{equation}
+For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
+\[
+ \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
+ V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
+ F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
+\]
+and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
+\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
+write
+\[
+ C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
+             =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
+\]
+with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
+ A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
+integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
+densities
+\[
+ k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
+                    A_s^\lambda(x/s).
+\]
+The nonnegative raw-tail defect energy
+\begin{align}
+ \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
+ &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
+ \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
+\end{align}
+is finite, and, with
+\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+  +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
+\end{equation}
+In particular, law by law,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
+ s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
+ \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
+ s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
+\end{equation}
+No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
+order \(r\), is required.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
+\(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
+\(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
+Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
+translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
+\begin{align}
+ &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
+ +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
+  \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,                                    
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
+ &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
+ +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le C_r|z|^j,qquad 1\le j\le r,                              
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
+\end{align}
+where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
+zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
+derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
+\(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
+to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
+\eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
+
+Set
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
+\]
+Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
+and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
+ q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
+minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
+\(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
+\[
+ s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
+ \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
+\]
+The same last estimate gives
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
+ \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+
+Translation preserves mass, so
+\[
+ V_s^\lambda\ge0,qquad
+ \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
+\]
+Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
+derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Since
+\(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=o(1)\), these facts and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} prove the asserted positivity and
+normalization of \(A_s^\lambda\).  Finiteness of the entropy terms follows
+as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}: the
+stable translation entropy is bounded by
+\(C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\), which is integrable under the finite first
+moment implied by \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}.
+
+It remains to separate the leading modes from two different tail
+backgrounds.  We record the needed estimate directly.  Put
+\(\epsilon=s^{-r}\), \(U_\lambda=\epsilon B_r^\lambda\), and abbreviate
+\(A_\nu=A_s^\nu\), \(A_\eta=A_s^\eta\).  All these backgrounds, as well as
+\(q_s^\lambda\), are bounded below by a positive constant for large \(s\).
+For the last assertion, the bounded log-gradient of \(p_1\) and Jensen's
+inequality give
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)\ge
+ \exp\{-\|\nabla\log p_1\|_\infty\EE_\lambda|X|/s\}\longrightarrow1
+\]
+uniformly in \(y\).  Since
+\(\int(V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon)\), the mean-value
+theorem for
+\(f(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\), together with
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}, yields
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders}
+ \int f(q_s^\nu,q_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int f(A_\nu+U_\nu,A_\eta+U_\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+  +o(\epsilon^2).
+\end{equation}
+Explicitly, along the joining segments both first derivatives of \(f\) are
+bounded by
+\(C\{\epsilon+V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta\}\); integration bounds the error by
+\[
+ C\epsilon(\|R_s^\nu\|_1+\|R_s^\eta\|_1)
+ +C(\|R_s^\nu\|_\infty+\|R_s^\eta\|_\infty)
+   (\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

 condition: it neither replaces the nonlinear tail energy by a moment nor
 assumes regular variation.
 \end{remark}
+
+The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
+potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
+difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
+difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
+nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
+\label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
+Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
+\(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
+ m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
+ \qquad
+ \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
+\end{equation}
+For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
+\[
+ \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
+ V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
+ F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
+\]
+and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
+\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
+write
+\[
+ C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
+             =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
+\]
+with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
+ A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
+integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
+densities
+\[
+ k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
+                    A_s^\lambda(x/s).
+\]
+The nonnegative raw-tail defect energy
+\begin{align}
+ \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
+ &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
+ \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
+\end{align}
+is finite, and, with
+\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+  +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
+\end{equation}
+In particular, law by law,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
+ s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
+ \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
+ s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
+\end{equation}
+No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
+order \(r\), is required.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
+\(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
+\(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
+Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
+translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
+\begin{align}
+ &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
+ +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
+  \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,                                    
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
+ &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
+ +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le C_r|z|^j,qquad 1\le j\le r,                              
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
+\end{align}
+where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
+zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
+derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
+\(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
+to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
+\eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
+
+Set
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
+\]
+Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
+and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
+ q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
+minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
+\(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
+\[
+ s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
+ \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
+\]
+The same last estimate gives
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
+ \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+
+Translation preserves mass, so
+\[
+ V_s^\lambda\ge0,qquad
+ \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
+\]
+Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
+derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Since
+\(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=o(1)\), these facts and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} prove the asserted positivity and
+normalization of \(A_s^\lambda\).  Finiteness of the entropy terms follows
+as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}: the
+stable translation entropy is bounded by
+\(C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\), which is integrable under the finite first
+moment implied by \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}.
+
+It remains to separate the leading modes from two different tail
+backgrounds.  We record the needed estimate directly.  Put
+\(\epsilon=s^{-r}\), \(U_\lambda=\epsilon B_r^\lambda\), and abbreviate
+\(A_\nu=A_s^\nu\), \(A_\eta=A_s^\eta\).  All these backgrounds, as well as
+\(q_s^\lambda\), are bounded below by a positive constant for large \(s\).
+For the last assertion, the bounded log-gradient of \(p_1\) and Jensen's
+inequality give
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)\ge
+ \exp\{-\|\nabla\log p_1\|_\infty\EE_\lambda|X|/s\}\longrightarrow1
+\]
+uniformly in \(y\).  Since
+\(\int(V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon)\), the mean-value
+theorem for
+\(f(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\), together with
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}, yields
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders}
+ \int f(q_s^\nu,q_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int f(A_\nu+U_\nu,A_\eta+U_\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+  +o(\epsilon^2).
+\end{equation}
+Explicitly, along the joining segments both first derivatives of \(f\) are
+bounded by
+\(C\{\epsilon+V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta\}\); integration bounds the error by
+\[
+ C\epsilon(\|R_s^\nu\|_1+\|R_s^\eta\|_1)
+ +C(\|R_s^\nu\|_\infty+\|R_s^\eta\|_\infty)
+   (\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

+At every order \(r\), finite \(r\)th moments alone also give a law-by-law
+decomposition: the universal quadratic tensor term plus a nonnegative
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy between two positive proxies that retain each law's
+unexpanded tail beyond the smoothing scale and their common lower-order jet.
+Vanishing of this tail-defect energy is an exact necessary and sufficient

 kernel, the Cayley transform additionally yields the exact equivalence between
 finite variance and bounded \(t^4\) relative entropy, an all-order finite

--- a/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex
+++ b/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex
@@ -506,3 +506,218 @@
 condition: it neither replaces the nonlinear tail energy by a moment nor
 assumes regular variation.
 \end{remark}
+
+The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
+potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
+difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
+difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
+nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
+\label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
+Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
+\(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
+ m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
+ \qquad
+ \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
+\end{equation}
+For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
+\[
+ \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
+ V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
+ F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
+\]
+and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
+\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
+write
+\[
+ C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
+             =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
+\]
+with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
+ A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
+integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
+densities
+\[
+ k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
+                    A_s^\lambda(x/s).
+\]
+The nonnegative raw-tail defect energy
+\begin{align}
+ \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
+ &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
+ \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
+\end{align}
+is finite, and, with
+\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+  +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
+\end{equation}
+In particular, law by law,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
+ s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
+ \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
+ s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
+\end{equation}
+No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
+order \(r\), is required.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
+\(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
+\(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
+Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
+translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
+\begin{align}
+ &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
+ +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
+  \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
+ &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
+ +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le C_r|z|^j,\qquad 1\le j\le r,
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
+\end{align}
+where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
+zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
+derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
+\(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
+to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
+\eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
+
+Set
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
+\]
+Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
+and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
+ q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
+minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
+\(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
+\[
+ s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
+ \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
+\]
+The same last estimate gives
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
+ \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+
+Translation preserves mass, so
+\[
+ V_s^\lambda\ge0,qquad
+ \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
+\]
+Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
+derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Since
+\(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=o(1)\), these facts and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} prove the asserted positivity and
+normalization of \(A_s^\lambda\).  Finiteness of the entropy terms follows
+as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}: the
+stable translation entropy is bounded by
+\(C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\), which is integrable under the finite first
+moment implied by \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}.
+
+It remains to separate the leading modes from two different tail
+backgrounds.  We record the needed estimate directly.  Put
+\(\epsilon=s^{-r}\), \(U_\lambda=\epsilon B_r^\lambda\), and abbreviate
+\(A_\nu=A_s^\nu\), \(A_\eta=A_s^\eta\).  All these backgrounds, as well as
+\(q_s^\lambda\), are bounded below by a positive constant for large \(s\).
+For the last assertion, the bounded log-gradient of \(p_1\) and Jensen's
+inequality give
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)\ge
+ \exp\{-\|\nabla\log p_1\|_\infty\EE_\lambda|X|/s\}\longrightarrow1
+\]
+uniformly in \(y\).  Since
+\(\int(V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon)\), the mean-value
+theorem for
+\(f(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\), together with
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}, yields
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders}
+ \int f(q_s^\nu,q_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int f(A_\nu+U_\nu,A_\eta+U_\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+  +o(\epsilon^2).
+\end{equation}
+Explicitly, along the joining segments both first derivatives of \(f\) are
+bounded by
+\(C\{\epsilon+V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta\}\); integration bounds the error by
+\[
+ C\epsilon(\|R_s^\nu\|_1+\|R_s^\eta\|_1)
+ +C(\|R_s^\nu\|_\infty+\|R_s^\eta\|_\infty)
+   (\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

 \begin{maintheorem}{Domain and endpoint closure for Cauchy relative entropy}
diff --git a/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex b/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex
