 condition: it neither replaces the nonlinear tail energy by a moment nor
 assumes regular variation.
 \end{remark}
+
+The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
+potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
+difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
+difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
+nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
+\label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
+Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
+\(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
+ m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
+ \qquad
+ \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
+\end{equation}
+For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
+\[
+ \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
+ V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
+ F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
+\]
+and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
+\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
+write
+\[
+ C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
+             =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
+\]
+with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
+ A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
+integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
+densities
+\[
+ k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
+                    A_s^\lambda(x/s).
+\]
+The nonnegative raw-tail defect energy
+\begin{align}
+ \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
+ &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
+ \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
+\end{align}
+is finite, and, with
+\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+  +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
+\end{equation}
+In particular, law by law,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
+ s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
+ \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
+ s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
+\end{equation}
+No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
+order \(r\), is required.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
+\(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
+\(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
+Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
+translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
+\begin{align}
+ &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
+ +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
+  \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
+ &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
+ +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
+ \le C_r|z|^j,\qquad 1\le j\le r,
+ \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
+\end{align}
+where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
+zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
+derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
+\(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
+to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
+\eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
+
+Set
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
+\]
+Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
+and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
+ q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
+\end{equation}
+Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
+minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
+ \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
+\(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
+\[
+ s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
+ \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
+\]
+The same last estimate gives
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
+ \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
+\end{equation}
+
+Translation preserves mass, so
+\[
+ V_s^\lambda\ge0,\qquad
+ \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
+\]
+Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
+derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Since
+\(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=o(1)\), these facts and
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} prove the asserted positivity and
+normalization of \(A_s^\lambda\).  Finiteness of the entropy terms follows
+as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}: the
+stable translation entropy is bounded by
+\(C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\), which is integrable under the finite first
+moment implied by \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}.
+
+It remains to separate the leading modes from two different tail
+backgrounds.  We record the needed estimate directly.  Put
+\(\epsilon=s^{-r}\), \(U_\lambda=\epsilon B_r^\lambda\), and abbreviate
+\(A_\nu=A_s^\nu\), \(A_\eta=A_s^\eta\).  All these backgrounds, as well as
+\(q_s^\lambda\), are bounded below by a positive constant for large \(s\).
+For the last assertion, the bounded log-gradient of \(p_1\) and Jensen's
+inequality give
+\[
+ q_s^\lambda(y)\ge
+ \exp\{-\|\nabla\log p_1\|_\infty\EE_\lambda|X|/s\}\longrightarrow1
+\]
+uniformly in \(y\).  Since
+\(\int(V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon)\), the mean-value
+theorem for
+\(f(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\), together with
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}, yields
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders}
+ \int f(q_s^\nu,q_s^\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int f(A_\nu+U_\nu,A_\eta+U_\eta)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+  +o(\epsilon^2).
+\end{equation}
+Explicitly, along the joining segments both first derivatives of \(f\) are
+bounded by
+\(C\{\epsilon+V_s^\nu+V_s^\eta\}\); integration bounds the error by
+\[
+ C\epsilon(\|R_s^\nu\|_1+\|R_s^\eta\|_1)
+ +C(\|R_s^\nu\|_\infty+\|R_s^\eta\|_\infty)
+   (\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+
+Now expand the last integral in \(t\) along
+\((A_\nu+tU_\nu,A_\eta+tU_\eta)\), \(0\le t\le1\).  Its first derivative
+at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{U_\nu\log\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}
+ +U_\eta\left(1-\frac{A_\nu}{A_\eta}\right)\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=o(\epsilon^2),
+\]
+because the coefficients are bounded by \(C|A_\nu-A_\eta|\) and
+\(\|A_\nu-A_\eta\|_1=o(\epsilon)\).  The second derivative at zero is
+\[
+ \int\left\{\frac{U_\nu^2}{A_\nu}
+ -\frac{2U_\nu U_\eta}{A_\eta}
+ +\frac{A_\nu U_\eta^2}{A_\eta^2}\right\}
+ \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
+ =\int(U_\nu-U_\eta)^2\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(\epsilon^2).
+\]
+To justify the last equality, use the uniform lower bounds and
+\[
+ \|A_\lambda-1\|_1
+ \le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty+2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
+\]
+The third derivative is bounded in absolute value by
+\(C\epsilon^3(1+A_\nu)\), whose integral is \(O(\epsilon^3)\).
+Taylor's theorem and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remove-remainders} therefore
+give
+\[
+ D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
+ =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+ +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
+       \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
+\]
+By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
+\(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
+\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
+is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
+\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
+
+The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
+convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
+The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
+lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
+unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
+relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
+centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
+which differs from the energy in
+Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
+in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
+\end{proof}

The arbitrary-order law-by-law nonnegative tail-defect decomposition holds.

**Exact Result**

I added one theorem, “Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition,” in [sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex:516), with the complete proof beginning at [line 575](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex:575).

Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), \(r\ge1\), and let \(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\mathbb R^d\) such that

\[
m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad (|\gamma|<r),
\qquad
\int |x|^r(\nu+\eta)(dx)<\infty.
\]

No nonzero assumption on \(\Delta_r\) is needed. Define the raw exterior potentials

\[
V_s^\lambda(y)
=
\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
\frac{p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y-x/s)}
     {p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y)}
\,\lambda(dx),
\qquad
\tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),
\]

and retain the common Taylor jet through order \(r-1\),

\[
C_{r-1,s}
=
\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu
=
\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta.
\]

The positive tail-jet proxy quotients are

\[
A_s^\lambda
=
1+C_{r-1,s}+V_s^\lambda-\tau_s^\lambda.
\]

For all sufficiently large \(s\), these define probability densities

\[
k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)
=
s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)A_s^\lambda(x/s).
\]

The tail-defect energy is the genuine relative \(\Phi\)-entropy

\[
\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
=
D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)
=
\int A_s^\eta
\Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
\,d\Omega_{\alpha,d},
\]

so it is finite and nonnegative. The proved decomposition is

\[
D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
=
\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
+\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
+o(s^{-2r}).
\]

Consequently, law by law,

\[
s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
\longrightarrow
\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
\iff
s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
\]

Thus both requested features survive at arbitrary order: the remainder is nonnegative, and its vanishing is an exact necessary and sufficient condition. The assumption is only a finite \(r\)-th absolute moment, rather than the stronger uniform sufficient moment \(p_{r,\alpha,d}\) used by the all-order asymptotic theorem.

**What Survived And What Was New**

The order-two proof was located in [sec_covariance_proxy_defect.tex](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_covariance_proxy_defect.tex:176) and, for general stable kernels, in the preceding stable law-by-law theorem at [sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_verified_A2_results_part_03.tex:332).

The following parts carry over directly:

- Split each quotient into its Taylor jet, its unexpanded exterior translate mixture, the removed tail mass, and a remainder.
- Use finite \(r\)-th moment dominated convergence to prove the interior remainder is \(o(s^{-r})\) in \(L^\infty\cap L^1\).
- Use
  \[
  s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\,d\lambda
  \le
  s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\,d\lambda=o(s^{-r})
  \]
  for every \(j\le r\).
- Use translation preservation of mass to obtain \(V_s^\lambda\ge0\) and \(\int V_s^\lambda\,d\Omega=\tau_s^\lambda\).
- Use \(\tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r})\) to make all interactions with omitted tail mass negligible at scale \(s^{-2r}\).

The genuinely new issue is that, for two laws and \(r\ge3\), \(V_s^\nu-V_s^\eta\) is signed. Applying \(\Phi\) directly to that difference can leave the domain \((-1,\infty)\) and cannot provide structural nonnegativity. The new argument retains the common lower-order jet in both positive tail proxies and expands relative entropy around two different tail backgrounds. The first variation is \(o(s^{-2r})\), the Hessian produces

\[
\frac12s^{-2r}\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2\,d\Omega
=
\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r},
\]

and the third-order remainder is \(O(s^{-3r})\). This two-background expansion is the only new mechanism required beyond the order-two proof.

For \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and centred \(\nu\), the new energy becomes \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\,d\Omega\), differing from the existing one-sided energy \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu)\,d\Omega\) by \(o(s^{-4})\). Thus it recovers the existing decomposition at its stated precision.

The abstract and Main Theorem B discussion were updated in [main.tex](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/main.tex:116) and [sec_introduction.tex](/D:/omega/automath/papers/publication/2026_cayley_chebyshev_poisson_entropy_strip_rkhs_jfa/sec_introduction.tex:160). No existing theorem was weakened or renumbered.
